        Question: How does Newton define 'quantity of motion' and how is it distinct from 'force'?

        Original Answer: Newton defines quantity of motion as the product of a body's mass and its velocity; force is that which changes this quantity—either resisting or impressing motion.

        Question: According to Newton, what are the three laws of motion and how do they apply in celestial mechanics?

        Original Answer: 1) A body remains at rest or in uniform motion unless acted on by an impressed force. 2) The change of motion is proportional to the impressed force and occurs in its direction. 3) For every action, there is an equal and opposite reaction. In celestial mechanics, these laws explain orbital persistence, gravitational acceleration, and mutual perturbations among bodies.

        Question: How does Newton derive Kepler's Second Law (equal areas in equal times) from his laws of motion and gravitation?

        Original Answer: He shows that a body under a central (centripetal) force sweeps out equal areas in equal times because the force always acts along the radius, altering direction but preserving area–time proportionality.

        Original Answer: The implanted prosthetic device is payable; external aids such as a walker or lumbar belt are excluded.

        Question: If an insured undergoes Intra Operative Neuro Monitoring (IONM) during brain surgery, and also needs ICU care in a city over 1 million population, how are the respective expenses limited according to modern treatments, critical care definition, and policy schedule?

        Original Answer: IONM is covered up to its sub-limit under Modern Treatments; ICU charges are capped at 4% of the Sum Insured per day and require a hospital with ≥15 beds in cities over 1 million.

        Question: A policyholder requests to add their newly-adopted child as a dependent. The child is 3 years old. What is the process and under what circumstances may the insurer refuse cover for the child, referencing eligibility and addition/deletion clauses?

        Original Answer: Notify insurer/TPA within 30 days of adoption with proof and prorated premium; coverage may be refused if relationship proof is inadequate or deposit insufficient.

        Question: While checking the process for submitting a dental claim for a 23-year-old financially dependent daughter (who recently married and changed her surname), also confirm the process for updating her last name in the policy records and provide the company's grievance redressal email.

        Original Answer: A female child ceases to be eligible on marriage, so a dental claim for a 23-year-old who's married cannot be admitted. To update her new surname or any family-status change, notify your insurer or TPA in writing with a copy of her marriage certificate as an endorsement. For any grievance, email csd@orientalinsurance.co.in.

Hospitalization Expenses
Hospitalization Coverage & Reimbursement Hospitalization coverage for medical/surgical treatment in Indian hospitals and nursing homes
* Medical/surgical treatment in registered hospitals
* Qualified medical practitioner advice required
* Coverage limited to sum insured amount
Domiciliary Hospitalization
Home treatment coverage when hospital admission not possible circumstances.
Minimum three Days treatment required.
Hospital Admission not possible
Qualified practitioner supervised treatment.
Reimbursement Process
* Reimbursement paid to hospitals or insured persons directly
* Network hospitals with prior consent
* Direct payment to insured
* Sum insured policy limits
Slide 5: Reimbursable Medical Expenses Coverage
Room Services
* Room and nursing expenses up to 1% of sum insured or ₹5,000 per day
* ICU expenses coverage
Medical Fees
* Surgeon, anesthetist, consultant specialist fees
* Anesthesia, blood, oxygen expenses
* Operation theater charges
* Medicines
Additional Services
* Ambulance services up to 1% of sum insured or ₹2,000 organ-donation hospitalization expenses coverage
Slide 6: Domiciliary Hospitalization Benefits
Coverage Definition
Home treatment coverage for over three days when hospital admission is not feasible
* Patient condition prevents hospital transfer
* Hospital room unavailability circumstances
* Minimum three days’ treatment required
Important Exclusions
* Specific conditions and expenses not covered under domiciliary hospitalization benefits
* Pre- and post-hospitalization expenses excluded
* Asthma, diabetes, hypertension specific diseases
* Coverage limit specified in policy schedule
Slide 7: Telemedicine & Maternity Benefits
Telemedicine Coverage
* Digital consultation expenses with registered medical practitioners via telecommunication platforms
* Covered up to ₹2,000 per insured family per policy period
Maternity Benefits
* Optional coverage with 10% additional premium for comprehensive maternal care
* First two children covered up to ₹50,000 each
* Nine-month waiting period required
* Newborn covered from day one
Slide 8: Specialized Medical Coverage Areas
HIV/AIDS
* Comprehensive coverage including acute infection, clinical latency, and AIDS-related medical treatment expenses
Mental Illness
* Hospitalization in mental-health establishments
* Excludes substance-abuse and mental-retardation therapies
Advanced Procedures
* Uterine Artery Embolization: ₹50,000
* Robotic Surgeries: ₹100,000
* Oral Chemotherapy: 25% of treatment cost
Slide 9: Important Policy Definitions
Hospital Facilities
* Registered facility with qualified nursing staff and medical practitioners
* Minimum 10–15 beds equipped with an operation theater
AYUSH Hospitals
* Facilities providing Ayurveda, Yoga, Unani, Siddha, Homeopathy treatments
* Qualified traditional medicine practitioners
Treatment Types
* Day-care procedures completed in a single day (e.g., dialysis, chemotherapy)
* In-patient: minimum 24-hour stay
Slide 10: Exclusions & Waiting Periods
Pre-existing Diseases Waiting
* 36-month waiting period for all pre-existing medical conditions before coverage becomes effective
Specified Diseases Timeline
* 1–3 years waiting periods for specific procedures (e.g., hernia, cataract, joint replacement)
General Waiting Period
* 30-day waiting period for all non-accident related illnesses before coverage activation
Slide 11: General Policy Exclusions
Non-Covered Expenses
* Specified medical expenses and treatments not covered
* Diagnostic evaluation-only hospital admissions
* Cosmetic surgery unless medically necessary
* Hazardous sports injuries treatment expenses
Other Exclusions: Additional exclusions including war nuclear attacks and non-medical expenses.
* War, nuclear, chemical attack related expenses
* Non-medical expenses (e.g., WiFi, toiletries)
* Medical treatments outside India
Slide 12: Policy Conditions & Requirements
Contract Claims: Essential policy contract terms and claim notification requirements for coverage
*  Policy proposal declarations and form complete the contract
* 48-hour claim notification required
* 7-day document submission deadline
Service Settlement: Cashless facility availability and claim settlement procedures for policyholders.
* Network hospitals cashless facility available
* 15-day claim settlement timeframe.
* Cancellation notice periods specified
Slide 13: Additional Policy Provisions & Benefits
Premium Adjustments
* Low-claim-ratio discount up to 40% premium reduction
* High-claim-ratio loading up to 150% increase
Policy Flexibility
* Portability, migration, transfer credits for pre-existing conditions
* Options to migrate to other insurers’ plans
Coverage Limits
* Moratorium period of 5 years
* Contestable period for fraud claims sum insured multiples from ₹50,000 to ₹500,000
Slide 14: Pre-Acceptance Health Checkup Requirements
Basic Tests
* Physical examination
* Urine test
* Glycated hemoglobin
* Ultrasonography
* ECG for ages 45+
